```
def _fft_convolve(a,b):
    n=len(a)+len(b)-1
    size=1<<(n-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    fb=np.fft.rfft(b,size)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,size)[:n]
```

For simplicity, for n<=1024 direct np.convolve might be faster than FFT in Python due overhead. But for many iterations, FFT maybe. We can choose:
```
def _conv(a,b=None):
    if b is None: b=a
    m=max(len(a),len(b))
    if m <= 2048:
        return np.convolve(a,b)
    size=1
    while size < len(a)+len(b)-1: size*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,size)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,size)[:len(a)+len(b)-1]
```
Direct for 512: np.convolve O(262k) C-level, very fast. For 1024: 1M, okay. For 2048: 4M, maybe okay if not many. Use threshold 2048.

Gradient from weights:
```
def _grad_from_weights(a,w, scale=1.0):
    # grad[scale] = 2 * sum_l a_l w_{l+j}
    n=len(a)
    if len(w) != 2*n-1: adjust?
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    c = _conv(a,w_rev)
    return 2.0 * c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1] * scale
```

def _grad_hard_max(a,k):
    n=len(a); g=np.zeros(n)
    lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
    # grad[j]=2*a[k-j] for j in lo..hi
    idx=np.arange(lo,hi+1); g[idx]=2*a[k-idx]; return g
```

Let's implement custom smooth gradient descent with Adam and line search. It can run thousands iterations quickly.

Pseudo:
```
def _score(a):
    S = a.sum()
    if S<1e-12: return inf
    b = _conv(a,a)
    return float(2*len(a)*b.max()/(S*S))

def _score_normalized(a): # sum1
    b=_conv(a,a); return 2*n*b.max
```
For optimization, fix sum=1 to avoid scale drift. Let a normalized. We can also use unnormalized and normalize after.

Softmax update:
```
def _smooth_grad_info(a, tau):
   b = _conv(a,a)
   # avoid overflow: m=b.max; shifted=b-m; exps=np.exp(shifted/tau); w=exps/exps.sum(); lse = tau*(np.log(exps.sum()) + m/tau)
   if tau <= 0: w=one-hot max; lse=m
   ga = _grad_from_weights(a,w)
   gx = ga - (a@ga)*a
   # objective 2n*lse? For exact score scale-invariant if sum1: Q=2n*lse (approx if smooth); for acceptance use exact.
   return lse, gx, b, w
```
If tau very small, w numeric one-hot; use hard.

Adam:
```
def _logspace_optimize(a0, deadline, tau0, tau1, max_iter):
    n=len(a0); a=a0/sum; x=np.log(a+1e-300); x-=x.mean()
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; best=a; best_score=_score(a)
    lr0=0.3
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time > deadline-...: break
       frac=it/max_iter; tau=tau0+(tau1-tau0)*frac; maybe cosine
       a=softmax(x)
       # If tau>1e-6:
       lse, gx, b = ...
       # if tau very small and gx zero? add top-k gradient
       m = 0.9*m + 0.1*gx; v=0.99*v+0.1*gx*gx
       step = (m/(sqrt(v)+1e-8))
       step -= step.mean()
       maxstep = np.max(np.abs(step)); if maxstep>0: step = step/maxstep
       # backtracking step sizes from base = lr0*(1/(1+sqrt(t)))? 
       f0=2*n*float(b.max()) # normalized exact
       for s in [0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625]: # scaled?
           x_new=x - s*step
           x_new-=x_new.mean()
           a_new=softmax(x_new)
           f_new=_score_normalized(a_new)
           if f_new < best_score: update best
           if f_new < f0*(1-1e-6):
               x=x_new; a=a_new; break
       else:
           # maybe tiny random perturbation
           x += 0.01*rng? Use deterministic? allow random with seed.
       # periodic recenter
    return best,best_score
```
But if f0 computed from b, exact normalized score. Use `_score_normalized` does conv; we already have b for current. Compute f0 = 2n*b.max.
Line search only tests few s. Adam m/v normalized; step size scale maybe. If no improvement, reduce or random.

def _line_search_blend(a,g, f0, n):
   best_f=f0; best_a=a; ts = [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9,1.0]

      if f<best_f: best_f=f; best_a=an
   return best_a,best_f

```
def _line_search(a,g,f0):
    ts = [1.0, 0.5, 0.75, 0.25, 0.9]
    best_f=f0; best_a=a
    for t in ts:
       an = (1-t)*a + t*g
       S=an.sum(); if S<1e-12 continue
       an/=S
       b=_conv(an,an); f=2n*b.max()
       if f<best_f: best_f=f; best_a=an; best_t=t
    # if best_t interior, local refine around best_t with 4 points
    if best_t not (1.0,0.0) and improvements:
       for t in [best_t-0.1,best_t-0.05,best_t+0.05,best_t+0.1]: ...
    return best_a,best_f
```

Let's implement `_lp_solve(a,M)` robust:
```
def _lp_solve(a,M):
    n=len(a)
    if M<=0: return None
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n))
    # Build columns
    # using for j in range(n): A[j:j+n,j]=a
    for j in range(n): A[j:j+n,j]=a
    c_obj = -np.ones(n)
    b_ub = np.full(2*n-1, M)
    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
    try:
       res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
       if res.status==0:
           x=res.x
           if x is not None and np.all(x>=-1e-9):
              return np.maximum(x,0)
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```
But building A with Python for j n times for 512 ->512 slice assignments, okay. For 1024 ->1024. Memory zero fill. Could preallocate and use `A.flat`? Slice assignment of contiguous column not contiguous; but numpy handles. Alternative dense A construction via `np.zeros` and loop rows maybe similar. For speed, if a is same only values change but zeros pattern fixed. Could precompute column slices? Not necessary.

vals = np.tile(a, n)
cols = np.tile(np.arange(n), n)? Need correct col for each (j,d): col=j. rows=j+d. For j=0..n-1, d=0..n-1: rows = np.repeat(np.arange(n), n), cols = np.repeat(np.arange(n)? A = coo_matrix((vals,(rows,cols)), shape=(2n-1,n)).tocsr()
```

def _lp_refine(a, deadline, target_score=None, max_no_improve_s=60.0):
    n=len(a); a=a.copy(); a=np.maximum(a,0); S=a.sum(); if S<...; a/=S
    best=a.copy(); best_f=_score(a)
    if target_score is None: target_score=1.45
    no_improve_start=time.monotonic()
    it=0; last_gradient=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 5:
       it+=1
       b=_conv(a,a); M=float(b.max()); f=2*n*M
       if f < best_f: best=a.copy(); best_f=f
       # If f near lower target? still
       # choose targets
       goal_M = target_score/(2*n)
       targets = []
       r_candidates = [1.0, 0.99, 0.98, 0.96, 0.92]
       # Always include current, then some smaller, then goal if lower
       for r in r_candidates:
           mt = M*r
           if mt > 0: targets.append(mt)
       if goal_M < M: targets.append(goal_M)
       # sort descending to try less aggressive first? If current M, it may return a -> no progress. Better try smaller first? But if too small g weird. We can try a few: start with r=0.99, 0.98, goal.
       targets = sorted(set(targets), reverse=False) # lowest first?
       # But if goal_M very low, LP maybe g sum tiny but line search may still improve; try before? We can try goal first, then 0.98, 1.0.
       tried=0
       improved_this_iter=False
       for mt in targets[:3]: # limit 3 LPs
           if time.monotonic() > deadline-5: break
           g=_lp_solve(a,mt)
           if g is None: continue
           Sg=g.sum()
           if Sg <= 1e-9: continue
           g /= Sg
           # If g is nearly same as a, maybe skip
           # line search
           a_new, f_new = _line_search(a,g,f)
           if f_new < best_f - 1e-12:
               best=a_new; best_f=f_new
           if f_new < f - 1e-12:
               a=a_new; improved_this_iter=True
               no_improve_start=time.monotonic()
               # break to recompute b with new a? yes
               break
       if not improved_this_iter:
           # try gradient perturbation every few no-improve
           if it % 5 == 0 or time.monotonic()-no_improve_start > 30:
               # do small log-adam step or random
               a = _smooth_step(a, tau=0.02, lr=0.1, direction='random'? )
               # ensure normalized
               if _score(a)<best_f: best...
       # If long no improvement, maybe change target or random restart? But within function, we can continue.
    return best,best_f
```

```
def _line_search(a,g,f0,max_points=8):
    cands = [1.0,0.5,0.75,0.25,0.9,0.1]
    best_f=f0; best_a=a; best_t=0.0
    for t in cands:
       if t==0: continue
       an = a + t*(g-a)  # since normalized sum, before clip
       S=an.sum()
       if S<1e-12: continue
       an = an/S
       b=_conv(an,an); f=2*n*b.max()
       if f<best_f: ...
    if best_t>0 and best_t<1 and improved:
       step = 0.25
       for dt in [0.1,0.05,-0.1,-0.05]: ...
    return best_a,best_f
```

```
def _softmax_weights(b, tau):
    if tau <= 1e-15:
       w=np.zeros_like(b); idx=np.argmax(b); w[idx]=1; return w
    m=b.max()
    e=np.exp((b-m)/tau)
    s=e.sum()
    if s==0 or not finite: one-hot
    return e/s
```

Now implement `_conv` robust:
```
_conv_cache = {}? For FFT norm maybe.
def _conv(a,b=None):
    if b is None: b=a
    la=len(a); lb=len(b); out_len=la+lb-1
    if out_len <= 4096: return np.convolve(a,b)
    size=1
    while size < out_len: size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    fb=np.fft.rfft(b,size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,size)
    return c[:out_len]
```
numpy direct for 4096 length maybe 16M, maybe okay; FFT threshold maybe 2048. Use threshold 2048. Need handle tiny imaginary: `c = np.fft.irfft(...)` real.

But for n=512 many calls, np.convolve direct is fast. For n=1024 direct 1M maybe also fast. Use threshold 2048. For 2048 direct 4M maybe maybe 0.01s? Python C direct maybe okay; but many iterations. Use FFT threshold 1024? FFT has overhead. Choose threshold 2048.

Let's implement `_grad_from_weights` as:
```
def _grad_from_weights(a,w):
    n=len(a)
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    c = _conv(a, w_rev)
    return 2.0 * c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```

def _grad_from_active(a, lags, weights):
   g=np.zeros(n)
   for k,wt in zip(lags,weights):
      lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
      idx=np.arange(lo,hi+1)
      g[idx] += wt * 2*a[k-idx]
   return g
```

Direct gradient line search:
```
def _gradient_step(a, tau_factor=0.05, lr=0.1):
   n=len(a); b=_conv(a,a); M=b.max(); f0=2*n*M
   if M<=0: return a,f0
   tau=tau_factor*M
   w=_softmax_weights(b,tau)
   ga=_grad_from_weights(a,w)
   ga -= ga.mean()
   gnorm=np.linalg.norm(ga)
   if gnorm<1e-15: return a,f0
   # scale direction so max abs = 1? Since a scale ~1/n, grad scale similar. Use normalized by n?
   # We'll try steps: alpha in [lr, lr/2,...]
   best_f=f0; best_a=a
   for step in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125]:
      an = a - lr*step*(ga/gnorm)
      an = np.maximum(an,0); S=an.sum()
      if S<1e-12: continue
      an/=S
      f=_score_norm(an)
      if f<best_f: best_f=f; best_a=an
   return best_a,best_f
```
But gradient direction ga/gnorm has scale 1; subtract lr*1 may be large relative to a values (1/n). For n=512, a values ~0.002, lr=0.1 huge -> negative all; clipping bad. For n=512, lr=0.1 changes ~0.0002, okay. But if τ small ga perhaps similar. Use raw. For line search try factors.
`ga -= ga.mean();` Since sum constraint. If ga has negative/positive. `an=a - lr*factor*ga`. Factor start maybe 1. Use lr around 1? Because ga magnitude 1/n, an values 1/n; an - lr*ga relative lr. If lr=1, relative change O(1). Good. Let's set lr_base=1.0 and factors [1,0.5,...]. Or lr=0.5.

Precompute index arrays:
```
_conv_idx_cache = {}
def _get_conv_indices(n):
   if n not in cache:
      j=np.arange(n)
      d=np.arange(n)
      rows = np.repeat(j, n) + np.tile(d, n)
      cols = np.repeat(j, n)
      cache[n]=(rows,cols)
   return cache[n]
```
Memory for n=1024: rows,cols each 1M int64 8MB, total 16MB. For n=512 4MB. Good. Precompute on demand.

LP solve using indices:
```
def _lp_solve(a,M):
    n=len(a)
    M=float(M)
    if M<=0: return None
    try:
       rows, cols = _get_conv_indices(n)
       A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
       vals=np.tile(a, n)
       A[rows, cols] = vals
       c_obj = -np.ones(n)
       b_ub = np.full(2*n-1, M)
       # bounds as list
       bds = [(0.0, None)]*n
       res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bds=bds, method='highs')
       if res.status == 0:
           x=res.x
           x=np.where(x<0,0,x)
           s=x.sum()
           if s>1e-12:
              return x
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```
Advanced indexing requires rows,cols int arrays; if n advances, okay. But rows,cols generated once; for each LP, `vals = np.tile(a, n)`; note `a` maybe not contiguous; tile returns contiguous. The assignment `A[rows, cols] = vals` uses repeated fancy indexing; if rows/cols large, okay. It may be slower than column loop due overhead of array temporaries? But acceptable. Could use column loop to avoid large arrays? Let's compare: for n=512, 512 loops of slice assignment might be faster and less memory. But vectorized fancy maybe C-level indexing with 262k, likely fast. We can make adaptive:
```
if n <= 2048:
   rows,cols = _get_conv_indices(n); vals=np.tile(a,n); A[rows,cols]=vals
else:
   for j in range(n): A[j:j+n,j]=a
```
For n=512, both okay. We'll use maybe column loop always to avoid storing rows/cols? But 512 slice assignment maybe not too bad. Let's estimate: 512 Python iterations, each assigning length up to 512 with slicing; total 262k memory copy, overhead 512. Fine. 1024 iterations. Simpler no precompute. But we need A dense anyway.    if f<best update
   tau = M * (tau_start + (tau_end-tau_start)*frac) maybe if M>0.
   w=softmax(b,tau)
   ga=grad
   gx=ga - (a@ga)*a
   # center gx (should near zero? a@gx=0 by construction? a dot (a@ga a)= (a@ga)(a@a)=a@ga, so yes zero)
   # Adam:
   t+=1
   m=0.9*m+0.1*gx; v=0.99*v+0.1*gx*gx
   step=m/(sqrt(v)+1e-10)
   step-=step.mean()
   # Scale step: If step all zero, break.
   # Estimate max a scale =1/n. We want x update ~lr * step / mean(abs(step)) maybe.
   step_scale = np.max(np.abs(step))
   if step_scale>0: step=step/step_scale
   # Backtracking
   f0=f
   accepted=False
   for s in [lr, lr*0.5, lr*0.25, lr*0.125, lr*0.0625]:
       xn=x - s*step
       xn-=xn.mean()
       an=softmax(xn)
       bnew=conv(an,an); fnew=2n*bnew.max()
       if fnew < f0 - 1e-12:
           x=xn; a=an; f=fnew; accepted=True; break
   if not accepted:
       # maybe random small perturbation
       # Add deterministic pseudo-random: use rng? We can pass rng.
       pass
- Return best.

def _softmax(x):

Pseudo code detailed:
```
def _lp_refine(a, stop_time, target_score=1.49):
    n=len(a); a=_normalize(a.copy())
    best=a.copy(); best_f=_score_normal(a)
    f=best_f
    if n > 1024:
        # gradient only
        return _optimize_adam(a, stop_time, ...)
    # factors cycle
    factors=[0.98,0.95,0.99,1.0,0.97,1.0,0.94]
    idx=0; it=0; no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic() < stop_time - 3.0:
        it+=1
        b=_conv(a,a); M=float(b.max()); f=2.0*n*M
        if f < best_f: best_f=f; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0
        # compute goal_M
        goal_score = min(target_score, best_f*0.999)
        goal_M = goal_score/(2.0*n)
        # choose target M
        factor = factors[idx]; idx=(idx+1)%len(factors)
        mt = M*factor
        if goal_M > 0 and (factor <= 0.96 or it % 3 == 0): mt = max(goal_M, mt*0.97?) # hmm
        # Ensure mt < M if factor<1; if mt >= M*0.999 and no improve many, use goal_M
        if mt >= M*0.999 and it % 4 == 0:
            mt = max(goal_M, M*0.98)
        if mt <= 0: mt = M*0.9
        g = _lp_solve(a, mt)
        improved=False
        if g is not None:
            Sg=g.sum()
            if Sg>1e-12:
                g/=Sg
                a_new,f_new=_line_search(a,g,f)
                if f_new < f - 1e-12:
                    a=a_new; f=f_new; improved=True; no_improve=0
                if f_new < best_f - 1e-12:
                    best=a_new; best_f=f_new
        if not improved:
            # maybe try a line search between a and best? If best different? But a should track best? We set a improved; if LP found new best but not better than current f? impossible if f_new<f? It could be best but not desc? no.
            # gradient every few iterations
            if it % 10 == 0 or no_improve > 30:
                a_g,f_g=_gradient_step(a, tau_factor=0.05, lr=0.5)
                if f_g < f:
                    a=a_g; f=f_g
                if f_g < best_f:
                    best=a_g; best_f=f_g
            no_improve +=1
            # if stuck too long, perturb with small random mix
            if no_improve > 100:
                # deterministic random using np.random? use global? We can use a fixed rng inside? Need pass or use np.random.default_rng(it)
                r = np.random.default_rng(1234+it)
                v = r.random(n) + 0.1
                v = _normalize(v)
                a = (1.0-0.1)*a + 0.1*v
                a = _normalize(a)
                no_improve = 0
    return best, best_f
```

Could use target score best*0.999 (0.05% reduction) to ensure feasible; but 2% may be infeasible? Feasibility doesn't require a; g=0 always feasible, but max sum might be very small. If M target too low, g maybe correlates with inverse of C; still okay. Even if max sum small, shape may be extreme and line search can improve. But if g nearly zero after normalization? Sum could be 1e-6; still shape. If solver underflows, skip. Use threshold Sg>1e-8.

LP max sum can be unbounded if M large and C row zero, not.

Let's test LP for uniform a length3 normalized [1/3,1/3,1/3]. b=[1/9,2/9,1/3,2/9,1/9], M=1/3? Wait central b_1? n=3 c full length5: [1/9,2/9,1/3,2/9,1/9]. Objective=6/3=2. M=0.333. Target 0.98M=0.326. C_a matrix column j uniform 1/3 shifted. Constraints: (1/3)*sum of subset g_j ≤0.326. All constraints similar; max sum finite: row central has all three => sum g ≤0.978, so max sum <=0.978 (less than 1). g uniform with sum 0.978 normalized uniform, no improvement. LP won't help from uniform. Gradient needed. Good. For central one-sided, constraints rows not symmetric; g can increase.

Thus for some starts (uniform/random) LP may fail; gradient can help. Main starts should be nonuniform (central, power-law) where LP can act.

Could use random but not uniform: `a = central + noise` maybe.

Let's implement `_optimize_adam(a, stop_time, tau_start=0.5, tau_end=0.01, max_iter=10000, lr=0.1, eval_every=1)` carefully.

Use exact normalized score. For each iter, compute b and gradient. We can combine b, M. Softmax weights use tau factor of M. Since M may be ~1/n. Set tau = M * tau_factor. If tau_factor=0.1, weights focus peak; if 1.0, smooth.

Direction scaling: Use raw `gx` and backtracking step sizes `[step_scale, step_scale*0.5,...]` where `step_scale = lr / (np.max(np.abs(gx)) + eps) * scale?` We want x update size ~lr. If gx magnitude ~1/n? Let's compute raw gx from ga. ga magnitude? w sum1, a values ~1/n; each ga_j =2∑ a_l w_{l+j}; with ~n terms? Since w sum1, weighted average of a ~1/n, so ga~1/n. n=512 =>0.002. x update of 0.1 changes a by 10%, good. So `step = gx / (np.max(np.abs(gx))+eps)` gives direction with max 1; multiply lr. Good. We'll use lr=0.1 (relative x update). For high n, okay.

Adam state with normalized step? If we use Adam on normalized direction, m/v units dimensionless. Good.

Algorithm:
```
m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; lr0=0.1
for it:
   a=softmax(x)
   b=conv; M=b.max; f=2nM
   update best
   tau=M*tau_factor
   w=softmax(b,tau)
   ga=grad
   gx=ga - (a@ga)*a
   maxX=np.max(np.abs(gx))
   if maxX<1e-15: break
   step=gx/maxX
   step-=step.mean()
   # Adam
   t+=1
   m=0.9*m+0.1*(step)
   v=0.99*v+0.1*(step*step)
   adam_step = m/(sqrt(v)+1e-8)
   adam_step -= adam_step.mean()
   maxAdam=np.max(np.abs(adam_step))
   if maxAdam<=0: continue
   adam_step /= maxAdam
   # Try step sizes [lr0, lr0*0.5, lr0*0.25]
   for s in [lr0, lr0*0.5, lr0*0.25, lr0*0.1]:
      xn = x - s*adam_step
      xn -= xn.mean()
      an=softmax(xn)
      bn=conv(an,an); fn=2n*bn.max()
      if fn < f - 1e-12:
          x=xn; a=an; f=fn; break
   else:
      # if no improvement, maybe accept tiny random? or break if too many
```
Potential issue: If exact objective non-smooth, LSE gradient may not decrease exact f at first even if LSE would; line search using exact f may reject. But as tau small, okay. We can accept if fn <= f + 1e-9? Too permissive. We'll reject.
If no accepted for many iters, reduce tau? Maybe tau factor anneals down. If rejected, x unchanged; next iteration same gradient; tries smaller step; eventually accepts if gradient valid. If not, maybe direction bad due nonconvex; we can random perturb. Add `stall` counter; if >20, add random vector to x with small magnitude and reset Adam.
Random perturbation: Use `np.random.default_rng(0+it)` to create ` perturb = rng.normal(size=n)` normalized, x += 0.05*dir. This may help but deterministic.
But random in high-dim may not improve; only occasional.

For starts, we can run Adam for fixed time (e.g. 120s) then update best. Then if n<=1024, run LP refine on that result for remaining.

Given time, we can implement both, but code length grows. Need ensure final code not too buggy.

Detailed helper implementations:

1. `_normalize(a, target=1.0)`:
```
def _normalize(a, target=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        # return uniform?
        return np.full_like(a, target / a.size) if a.size else a
    return a * (target / s)
```
For empty? no.

2. `_score(a)`:
```
def _score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0: return inf
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-12: return inf
    b = _conv(a,a)
    M = float(b.max()) if b.size else 0.0
    n = a.size
    return float(2.0*n*M/(S*S))
```
`_score_norm(a)` assume normalized; but to avoid mistakes use `_score` everywhere? It includes sum, but for line search after normalizing, sum1; `_score_norm` avoids sum. We'll implement both.

```
def _score_norm(a):
    n=len(a); b=_conv(a,a); return float(2.0*n*float(np.max(b)))
```
If a sum not exactly 1 due clipping, score should use sum? In optimization we normalize, so use norm. For final, use `_score`.

3. `_conv`:
```
def _conv(a,b=None):
    if b is None: b=a
    la=len(a); lb=len(b)
    out_len = la+lb-1
    if out_len <= 2048:
        return np.convolve(a,b, mode='full')
    size=1
    while size < out_len: size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    fb=np.fft.rfft(b,size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,size)
    return c[:out_len]
```
Potential: For negative zeros? okay.

4. `_softmax_weights(b, tau)`:
```
def _softmax_weights(b, tau):
    m = float(b.max())
    if tau <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(m):
       w = np.zeros_like(b); if b.size: w[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0; return w
    e = np.exp((b - m) / tau)
    s = e.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
       w = np.zeros_like(b); w[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0; return w
    return e / s
```
Note b and m: if b values ~1e-3, tau~1e-4, ratio up to 10; safe.

5. `_grad_from_weights(a,w)`:
```
def _grad_from_weights(a,w):
    n=len(a)
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    c = _conv(a,w_rev)
    return 2.0 * c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```
Check slice end: For n=1, c len1, c[0:1] good. For n=2, c len4, c[1:3] indices1,2 reversed. Good.

6. `_line_search(a,g,f0)`:
```
def _line_search(a,g,f0):
    n=len(a)
    # quick diff check
    d = g - a
    if np.dot(d,d) < 1e-16:
        return a, f0
    best_f = f0
    best_a = a
    # Candidate t: 1.0, 0.5, 0.75, 0.25, 0.9, 0.1, 0.35, 0.65
    ts = (1.0,0.5,0.75,0.25,0.9,0.1,0.35,0.65)
    for t in ts:
        an = a + t*(g-a) # since g,a normalized; but after LP maybe g has negative clipped? yes
        s = float(an.sum())
        if s <= 1e-300: continue
        an = an / s
        b = _conv(an,an)
        f = float(2.0*n*np.max(b))
        if f < best_f:
            best_f = f; best_a = an; best_t = t
    # If best_t interior and improvement, small local search (optional, but line count enough)
    return best_a, best_f
```
Since a,g nonnegative, an nonnegative; no clip needed. But g may have tiny negatives? `_lp_solve` clips. Good.
We can track `best_t`; init 0. If best_t in (0,1) and f improved, refine with 4 points around best_t maybe optional. To save, skip or do if time. Let's add light refine:
```
if best_t >0 and best_t <1 and best_f < f0:
   for dt in (0.1,-0.1,0.05,-0.05,0.2,-0.2):
      t2=best_t+dt; if 0<t2<1: evaluate
```
This doubles for improved candidates. Could be okay. But many LPs; use only if time? We don't pass time. We can include but maybe not. Keep 8 points enough.

7. `_lp_solve(a,M)`. Need import `from scipy.optimize import linprog`. If SciPy missing? We can try except? The task says allowed. We'll import at top. If `linprog` fails, return None.
Potential memory: `A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n))`. For n=1024 16MB; fine. Column loop:
```
def _lp_solve(a, M):
    n=len(a)
    M=float(M)
    if M <= 0 or n == 0: return None
    try:
        A = np.zeros((2*n-1, n), dtype=np.float64)
        # column j: rows j..j+n-1 = a
        for j in range(n):
            A[j:j+n, j] = a
        c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        b_ub = np.full(2*n-1, M, dtype=np.float64)
        bds = [(0.0, None)]*n
        res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bds, method='highs')
        if res.status == 0 and res.x is not None:
            x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            x[x < 0.0] = 0.0
            s = float(x.sum())
            if s > 1e-12:
                return x
    except Exception:
        pass
    return None
```
Time: Creating `bds` list each solve. Could cache bounds per n? Use dict `_lp_bounds_cache`. But list fine. `c_obj` create each. Could cache. We'll add caches:
```
_LP_BOUNDS_CACHE = {}
def _get_lp_bounds(n):
   if n not in _LP_BOUNDS_CACHE: _LP_BOUNDS_CACHE[n]=[(0.0,None)]*n
   return _LP_BOUNDS_CACHE[n]
```
`c_obj` can be cached too `_LP_OBJ_CACHE[n] = -np.ones(n)`? But if linprog modifies? It shouldn't. Use copy? To be safe, pass `c_obj.copy()`. Not necessary? linprog may not modify. We can cache and pass same. Fine.

But if linprog is called with same A different M, bounds same.

Potential issue: The LP constraints correspond to Cg≤M with C columns as a. Let's verify again. For normalize a, g, conv(a,g)[m] = ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} a[m-j]g_j if valid. Constraint row m uses column j value a[m-j] if 0≤m-j<n. Column j rows m=j..j+n-1 values a[0..n-1]. Yes `A[j:j+n,j]=a`. Good.

If a and g lengths differ? both n.

8. `_lp_refine(a, stop_time, target_score=1.49)`:
Let's implement robust.
```
def _lp_refine(a_in, stop_time, target_score=1.49):
    a = np.array(a_in, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.maximum(a,0.0)
    a = _normalize(a)
    best = a.copy(); best_f = _score_norm(a)
    n=len(a)
    if n > 1024:
        # use adam
        return _adam_optimize(best, stop_time, tau_start=0.5, tau_end=0.02, lr=0.1, max_iter=1000000)
    factors = (0.98,0.96,0.99,0.97,1.0,0.95,0.98,0.94)
    idx=0; it=0; stall=0
    # maybe measure with first solve; no
    while time.monotonic() < stop_time - 3.0:
        it += 1
        b = _conv(a,a)
        M = float(b.max())
        f = 2.0*n*M
        if f < best_f - 1e-12:
            best_f = f; best = a.copy(); stall=0
        goal_score = min(target_score, best_f * 0.999)
        goal_M = goal_score / (2.0*n)
        factor = factors[idx]
        idx = (idx + 1) % len(factors)
        # If factor = 1 and stall high, use more aggressive
        mt = M * factor
        # If target score is lower than mt, maybe push to goal every few iterations
        if it % 3 == 0:
            mt = max(goal_M, M * 0.98)
            if goal_M < M * 0.9:
                mt = max(goal_M, M * (0.95 if (it//3)%2==0 else 0.93))
        # ensure positive
        if mt <= 0:
            mt = M*0.98
        g = None
        if M > 0:
            g = _lp_solve(a, mt)
        improved = False
        if g is not None:
            Sg = float(g.sum())
            if Sg > 1e-12:
                g = g / Sg
                a_new, f_new = _line_search(a, g, f)
                if f_new < f - 1e-12:
                    a = a_new
                    f = f_new
                    improved = True
                if f_new < best_f - 1e-12:
                    best_f = f_new
                    best = a_new.copy()
        if not improved:
            # second attempt with maybe current M (or goal) if time
            if it % 2 == 0 and time.monotonic() < stop_time - 5.0:
                mt2 = max(goal_M, M*0.995)
                if mt2 <= 0: mt2=M*0.98
                g2 = _lp_solve(a, mt2)
                if g2 is not None:
                    Sg2=float(g2.sum())
                    if Sg2>1e-12:
                        g2/=Sg2
                        a2,f2=_line_search(a,g2,f)
                        if f2 < f - 1e-12:
                            a=a2; f=f2; improved=True
                        if f2 < best_f - 1e-12:
                            best_f=f2; best=a2.copy()
            if not improved:
                stall += 1
                if stall == 20 or stall % 50 == 0:
                    # gradient step
                    ag,fg = _gradient_step(a, tau_factor=0.05, lr=0.5)
                    if fg < f:
                        a=ag; f=fg
                    if fg < best_f:
                        best_f=fg; best=ag.copy()
                    stall=0
                # if stuck long, perturb
                if stall >= 80:
                    r = np.random.default_rng(987654 + it)
                    v = r.random(n) * 0.5 + 0.05
                    v = _normalize(v)
                    a = _normalize(0.9*a + 0.1*v)
                    stall=0
    return best, best_f
```
Potential: If `best_f` = target_score (1.5) and `goal_score = min(1.49, 1.49995)` = 1.49, goal lower. If current f < target? We may still try to improve below 1.49. Good.
But if current f significantly above target, `goal_score=best*0.999` maybe not target if best close; if best far, target=1.49 lower. So goal pushes to 1.49. If target reached, still pushes 1.49? Maybe we want continue to 1.4 but evaluator target 1.5027; get as low as possible. We can set target_score=1.4 to encourage lower? But LP goals too low may produce poor g. We can set goal to best*0.999 mostly. Use `goal_score = best_f * 0.999` if `best_f > 1.45` else min(1.45, best_f*0.999). This gradually starts at best and lowers floor to 1.45. Let's set:
```
if best_f > 1.45:
    goal_score = best_f * 0.999
else:
    goal_score = min(1.45, best_f * 0.999)
```
But if best=1.52, goal=1.5185, not target; okay. It tightens gradually. The factor M*0.98 may be lower than goal if best improvement >2%, so factor controls. Good.

What if `_lp_solve` very slow for random? Time loop checks before, but solve can exceed stop. If stop_time is final with margin 3s, a slow LP near end could overrun. For 512 unlikely >3s. For 1024 maybe. We can add a hard `if n<=512` for frequent LP; for n=1024 limit solves: In `_lp_refine`, if n>768, do a first timed LP to estimate. But main can avoid long LP on 1024 near deadline. We'll set stop_time early for large n.

9. `_gradient_step(a, tau_factor=0.05, lr=0.5)`:
```
def _gradient_step(a, tau_factor=0.05, lr=0.5):
    n=len(a)
    b=_conv(a,a); M=float(b.max()); f0=2*n*M
    if M<=0: return a,f0
    tau = tau_factor*M
    w = _softmax_weights(b, tau)
    ga = _grad_from_weights(a, w)
    ga -= ga.mean()
    # If gradient zero, return
    if np.linalg.norm(ga) < 1e-15: return a,f0
    best_f=f0; best_a=a
    # Try factors
    for fct in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125):
        an = a - lr*fct*ga
        an = np.maximum(an,0.0)
        S=an.sum()
        if S<=1e-300: continue
        an/=S
        bn=_conv(an,an); fn=2*n*float(bn.max())
        if fn < best_f:
            best_f=fn; best_a=an
    return best_a,best_f
```
Will this improve? Maybe. `ga` magnitude ~1/n, lr=0.5 gives relative 0.5. Good. If an all zeros? S check.
Could use log-space if direct fails, but okay.

10. `_adam_optimize(a_in, stop_time, tau_start=0.5, tau_end=0.02, lr=0.1, max_iter=200000)`:
Implement as above. Need be efficient. Use local variables? no closures.
```
def _adam_optimize(a_in, stop_time, tau_start=0.5, tau_end=0.02, lr=0.1, max_iter=200000):
    a = np.asarray(a_in, dtype=np.float64).copy(); a=np.maximum(a,0); a=_normalize(a)
    n=len(a); best=a.copy(); best_f=_score_norm(a)
    x=np.log(a+1e-300); x-=x.mean()
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; order=1; stall=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < stop_time - 3.0 and it < max_iter:
       it+=1
       frac = 1.0 if it<max_iter else 1.0
       # use cosine from 0 to1 over iterations? But stop time unknown. Use it/max_iter.
       tau_factor = tau_start + (tau_end - tau_start) * min(1.0, it / max(1, max_iter//2))? Simpler: linear over 5000 iterations? Could use while. We don't know total. Use decay by it: `tau_factor = tau_end + (tau_start-tau_end)*np.exp(-0.02*it)`? Start 0.5, after 100 0.5*0.135? Maybe too fast. Use 0.005*it to 0.02.
       tau_factor = max(tau_end, tau_start * np.exp(-0.01*it))
       a = _softmax(x)
       b = _conv(a,a)
       M=float(b.max()); f=2*n*M
       if f < best_f: best_f=f; best=a.copy(); stall=0
       if M<=0: break
       tau = tau_factor*M
       w = _softmax_weights(b,tau)
       ga = _grad_from_weights(a,w)
       gx = ga - (a.dot(ga))*a
       gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(gx)))
       if gmax < 1e-15: break
       step = gx / gmax
       step -= step.mean()
       t += 1
       m = 0.9*m + 0.1*step
       v = 0.99*v + 0.1*(step*step)
       adam = m / (np.sqrt(v) + 1e-8)
       adam -= adam.mean()
       amax = float(np.max(np.abs(adam)))
       if amax < 1e-15: 
          # try raw step
          adam = step
       else:
          adam = adam / amax
       accepted=False
       for s in (lr, 0.5*lr, 0.25*lr, 0.125*lr, 0.0625*lr):
           xn = x - s*adam
           xn -= xn.mean()
           an = _softmax(xn)
           bn = _conv(an,an); fn = 2*n*float(bn.max())
           if fn < f - 1e-12:
               x=xn; a=an; f=fn; accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
           stall +=1
           if stall > 30:
               # random perturbation
               r = np.random.default_rng(1000000 + it)
               pert = r.standard_normal(n)
               pert -= pert.mean()
               pmax = float(np.max(np.abs(pert)))
               if pmax>0:
                   x = x + 0.05*(pert/pmax)
                   x -= x.mean()
                   m.fill(0); v.fill(0); t=0
               stall=0
    return best, best_f
```
Concern: `max_iter` default 200000; `tau_factor` decays by exp(-0.01*it): after 200 iter 0.08, after 500 0.007 reaches tau_end. Good.
Line search computes conv 5 times if fail; for time allocation, could be heavy. We can reduce to 3: `(lr, 0.5lr, 0.25lr)`. If fail, stall. Use 3.
Also, using exact objective; LSE gradient may not align. Could use LSE value for line search instead of exact to accept smoother, but final best exact. If we accept based on LSE, we might temporarily increase exact but later reduce. Could help. Let's accept if `fn_lse < f_lse` or `fn < f`. But computing LSE requires lofsumexp; we have w and b? After step, can recompute LSE; or just use exact. Simpler exact.
Maybe if exact not improve but LSE improves, take? Could implement:
- current lse = tau*(log sum e + m/tau) from b.
- candidate lse_new similarly? Need bnew and tau; compute maybe `np.logsumexp` manually. Extra cost. Could skip.
For gradient prepass, maybe direct step easier.

11. Constructors:
```
def _make_central_binomial(n):
    c=np.empty(n,float)
    c[0]=1.0
    for k in range(n-1):
       c[k+1]=c[k]*(2*k+1)/(2*k+2)
    return _normalize(c)
```
Check recurrence stable for n large: c decreases ~1/√k; no underflow for n=10000 (~0.005), fine.

```
def _make_power_law(n, alpha=0.45, beta=0.0, eps=1e-6):
    i=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)+1.0
    a = i**(-alpha)
    if beta !=0:
       r = (n - np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64))**beta
       a = a + 0.1*r? Maybe separate.
    a=np.maximum(a,1e-12)
    return _normalize(a)
```

```
def _make_mixed(n, left_alpha=0.5, right_alpha=0.05, weight=0.1):
    i=np.arange(n)+1
    left = i**(-left_alpha)
    right = (n-i+1)**(-right_alpha)
    a = left + weight*right
```
Right exponent small to avoid central blow. Good.

```
def _make_resample(seq, n):
    m=len(seq); if m==0 return central; if m==n return seq
    x_old=np.linspace(0,1,m); y=seq; x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n); z=np.interp(x_new,x_old,y); return _normalize(np.maximum(z,0))
```

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + total_budget - 15.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_seq = None; best_score = float('inf')

    # function to update? no nested; do manually.
    # Try global
    try:
       gs = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
       if gs.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(gs)):
           gs = np.clip(gs,0,1000)
           s = gs.sum()
           if s > 1e-12:
               cand = gs/s
               sc = _score(cand)
               if sc < best_score:
                   best_score = sc; best_seq = cand.copy()
    except Exception:
       pass
    if best_seq is None:
       cand = _make_central_binomial(512)
       best_seq = cand; best_score = _score(cand)
    n_best = len(best_seq)
    print(f"Initial best: n={n_best}, score={best_score:.8f}")

    # Phase 1: LP refine current best (if n<=1024) for some time.
    phase1_stop = min(deadline, t0 + 600.0)
    if n_best <= 1024:
        seq, sc = _lp_refine(best_seq, phase1_stop, target_score=1.49)
        if sc < best_score: update
    else:
        seq, sc = _adam_optimize(best_seq, phase1_stop)
        if sc < best_score: update

    # Phase 2: Central binomial 512 if different/better? If global best maybe already good. Always try central 512 with LP for 300s if time.
    phase2_stop = min(deadline, t0 + 1000.0)
    if time.monotonic() < phase2_stop - 30:
       cent = _make_central_binomial(512)
       # quick gradient to avoid uniform? not necessary
       seq, sc = _lp_refine(cent, phase2_stop, target_score=1.49)
       if sc < best_score: update

    # Phase 3: Try alternative starts lengths up to 2048 with Adam + maybe LP if n<=1024.
    start_spec = []
    # Use best sequence resampled to other lengths if n_best not huge
    for n in (512, 768, 1024, 1536, 2048):
       # maybe only up to remaining time/100
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 200: break
       if n == len(best_seq): continue?
       # create from central and power/mixed
       start_spec.append(_make_central_binomial(n))
       start_spec.append(_make_mixed(n, left_alpha=0.5, right_alpha=0.2, weight=0.05))
       if n_best > 0 and abs(n - len(best_seq)) <= len(best_seq):
           start_spec.append(_make_resample(best_seq, n))
    for start in start_spec:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 100: break
       n = len(start)
       # allocate time: 80s if n<=512, 120s if <=1024, 150s else
       if n <= 512: alloc=80.0
       elif n <=1024: alloc=120.0
       else: alloc=150.0
       stop = min(deadline - 50.0, time.monotonic() + alloc)
       if n <= 1024:
           seq, sc = _lp_refine(start, stop, target_score=1.49)
       else:
           seq, sc = _adam_optimize(start, stop)
       if sc < best_score: update
       # If a length >1024 finds good, maybe resample down? Maybe if sc < best_score? Already. If sc good and n>1024, we can later LP on downsample? But return best length; no need.

    # Phase 4: Final refinement on global best with remaining time, if n<=1024 use LP, else Adam.
    if best_seq is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 10:
       # If best is length 1024 and we have time >100, run LP; if length >1024, run Adam.
       alloc = max(60.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
       # For n>1024, maybe run Adam for alloc; for n<=1024, LP.
       if len(best_seq) <= 1024:
           seq, sc = _lp_refine(best_seq, deadline, target_score=1.49)
       else:
           seq, sc = _adam_optimize(best_seq, deadline)
       if sc < best_score: update
       # If there is a 512 version of a large best that may be LP-optimizable? Maybe resample best to 512 if best len !=512 and time >60: run LP on resample.
       if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30 and len(best_seq) != 512:
           cand = _make_resample(best_seq, 512)
           seq, sc = _lp_refine(cand, min(deadline, time.monotonic()+120.0), target_score=1.49)
           if sc < best_score: update

    # Final validation
    if best_seq is not None:
       out = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64)
       out = np.maximum(out,0.0)
       # maybe set too small to 0? not needed
       out = np.minimum(out, 1000.0)
       # normalize? Keep sum maybe 1. If sum <1e-12 fallback
       s=out.sum()
       if s < 1e-12:
           out = _make_central_binomial(512)
       # We can optionally renormalize to sum 1 (no effect score, but avoid huge values)
       out = out / s
       final_score = _score(out)
       print(f"Final computed score: {final_score:.8f}, n={len(out)}")
       try:
           es = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in out]))
           print(f"evaluate_sequence score: {es:.8f}")
           if es < final_score: final_score = es
       except Exception as e:
           print("evaluation error:", e)
       return [float(x) for x in out]
    # fallback
    fallback = _make_central_binomial(512)
    return [float(x) for x in fallback]
```

```
def _softmax_lse(b, tau):
    m=float(b.max())
    if tau <= 1e-15:
       w=zeros; w[argmax]=1; return w, m
    e=np.exp((b-m)/tau)
    se=e.sum()
    if se <=...:
       w one-hot; return w,m
    w=e/se
    lse = tau*(np.log(se)+m/tau)
    return w,lse
```

def _make_mixed(n, left_alpha=0.5, right_alpha=0.2, weight=0.05):
    i=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)+1.0
    left = np.power(i, -left_alpha)
    if right_alpha >=0:

       right = np.power(r, -right_alpha)

    else: ... not needed
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
    return _normalize(a)
```

Now, maybe use `height_sequence_1` to create starts by resampling. The resample function with linear interpolation might create smooth sequences; good.

```
def _make_resample(seq, n):
   m=len(seq)
   if m==0: return _make_central_binomial(n)
   if m==n: return _normalize(seq)
   xu = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
   xn = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   y = np.interp(xn, xu, seq)
   return _normalize(np.maximum(y,0.0))
```

          if sc < best_score: update

          seq, sc = _lp_refine(cand, min(deadline, time.monotonic()+80.0), target_score=1.48)
          if sc < best_score: update
    # final validate
    ...

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2 * n * max(np.convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a))**2.

The implementation combines:
  - robust initialization from one-sided "flat-convolution" seeds,
  - LP-based alternating refinement (maximize sum(g) subject to a*g <= M),
  - smoothed exact-objective gradient / log-Adam for stagnation escape.

All helpers are top-level. No nested functions or lambdas are used.
"""

import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import linprog

_LP_BOUNDS_CACHE = {}
_LP_OBJ_CACHE = {}


def _get_lp_bounds(n):
    if n not in _LP_BOUNDS_CACHE:
        _LP_BOUNDS_CACHE[n] = [(0.0, None)] * n
    return _LP_BOUNDS_CACHE[n]


def _get_lp_obj(n):
    if n not in _LP_OBJ_CACHE:
        _LP_OBJ_CACHE[n] = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    return _LP_OBJ_CACHE[n]


def _normalize(a, target=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.shape, float(target) / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a * (float(target) / s)


def _conv(a, b=None):
    if b is None:
        b = a
    la = len(a)
    lb = len(b)
    out_len = la + lb - 1
    if out_len <= 2048:
        return np.convolve(a, b, mode="full")
    size = 1
    while size < out_len:
        size <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    fb = np.fft.rfft(b, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, size)
    return c[:out_len]


def _score_from_conv(a, b):
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-12:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(b))
    return float(2.0 * len(a) * m / (S * S))


def _score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    b = _conv(a, a)
    return _score_from_conv(a, b)


def _softmax_lse(b, tau):
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    m = float(b.max())
    if tau <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(m):
        w = np.zeros_like(b)
        if w.size:
            w[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        return w, m
    e = np.exp((b - m) / tau)
    se = float(e.sum())
    if se <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(se):
        w = np.zeros_like(b)
        if w.size:
            w[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        return w, m
    w = e / se
    lse = float(tau * (np.log(se) + m / tau))
    return w, lse


def _grad_from_weights(a, w):
    n = len(a)
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    c = _conv(a, w_rev)
    return 2.0 * c[n - 1:2 * n - 1][::-1]


def _line_search(a, g, f0):
    n = len(a)
    d = g - a
    if float(np.dot(d, d)) < 1e-16:
        return a, f0

    best_f = float(f0)
    best_a = a

    candidates = (1.0, 0.5, 0.75, 0.25, 0.9, 0.35)
    for t in candidates:
        an = a + t * (g - a)
        s = float(an.sum())
        if s <= 1e-300:
            continue
        an = an / s
        b = _conv(an, an)
        f = _score_from_conv(an, b)
        if f < best_f:
            best_f = f
            best_a = an

    return best_a, best_f


def _lp_solve(a, M):
    n = len(a)
    M = float(M)
    if M <= 0 or n == 0:
        return None

    try:
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).astype(np.float64, copy=False)
        A = np.zeros((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
        for j in range(n):
            A[j:j + n, j] = a

        c_obj = _get_lp_obj(n).copy()
        b_ub = np.full(2 * n - 1, M, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = _get_lp_bounds(n)

        try:
            res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method="highs")
        except Exception:
            res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method="interior-point")

        if res.status == 0 and res.x is not None:
            x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            x[x < 0.0] = 0.0
            s = float(x.sum())
            if s > 1e-12:
                return x
    except Exception:
        pass

    return None


def _gradient_step(a, tau_factor=0.05, lr=0.5):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    b = _conv(a, a)
    f0 = _score_from_conv(a, b)

    M = float(b.max())
    if M <= 0:
        return a, f0

    tau = tau_factor * M
    w, _ = _softmax_lse(b, tau)
    ga = _grad_from_weights(a, w)
    ga = ga - ga.mean()
    if np.linalg.norm(ga) < 1e-15:
        return a, f0

    best_f = f0
    best_a = a
    for fct in (1.0, 0.7, 0.4, 0.2, 0.1):
        an = a - lr * fct * ga
        an = np.maximum(an, 0.0)
        s = float(an.sum())
        if s <= 1e-300:
            continue
        an = an / s
        b2 = _conv(an, an)
        f2 = _score_from_conv(an, b2)
        if f2 < best_f:
            best_f = f2
            best_a = an

    return best_a, best_f


def _adam_optimize(a_in, stop_time, tau_start=0.5, tau_end=0.02, lr=0.1):
    a = np.asarray(a_in, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    a = _normalize(a)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return a, float("inf")

    best = a.copy()
    best_f = _score(a)

    x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    x -= x.mean()
    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 0
    it = 0
    stall = 0

    while time.monotonic() < stop_time - 5.0:
        it += 1
        frac = min(1.0, float(it) / 2000.0)
        tau_factor = tau_start + (tau_end - tau_start) * frac

        a = np.exp(x - x.max())
        a /= a.sum()

        b = _conv(a, a)
        f = _score_from_conv(a, b)
        if f < best_f:
            best_f = f
            best = a.copy()
            stall = 0

        if f > best_f * 1.25:
            a = best.copy()
            x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
            x -= x.mean()
            m.fill(0.0)
            v.fill(0.0)
            t = 0
            stall = 0
            continue

        M = float(b.max())
        if M <= 0:
            break

        tau = max(1e-18, tau_factor * M)
        w, lse = _softmax_lse(b, tau)
        ga = _grad_from_weights(a, w)
        gx = ga - (float(a.dot(ga)) * a)
        gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(gx)))
        if gmax < 1e-15:
            break

        step = gx / gmax
        step -= step.mean()

        t += 1
        m = 0.9 * m + 0.1 * step
        v = 0.99 * v + 0.1 * (step * step)
        adam = m / (np.sqrt(v) + 1e-8)
        adam -= adam.mean()
        amax = float(np.max(np.abs(adam)))
        if amax < 1e-15:
            adam = step
        else:
            adam = adam / amax

        lr_t = lr / (1.0 + 0.002 * float(it))
        accepted = False
        for step_size in (lr_t, 0.5 * lr_t, 0.25 * lr_t):
            xn = x - step_size * adam
            xn -= xn.mean()
            an = np.exp(xn - xn.max())
            an /= an.sum()
            bn = _conv(an, an)
            fn = _score_from_conv(an, bn)
            _, lse_new = _softmax_lse(bn, tau)

            if (fn < f - 1e-12) or (lse_new < lse - 1e-10 and fn < f + 0.02):
                x = xn
                a = an
                f = fn
                lse = lse_new
                accepted = True
                if fn < best_f:
                    best_f = fn
                    best = an.copy()
                break

        if not accepted:
            stall += 1
            if stall > 30:
                r = np.random.default_rng(1000003 + it)
                pert = r.standard_normal(n)
                pert -= pert.mean()
                pmax = float(np.max(np.abs(pert)))
                if pmax > 0:
                    x = x + 0.05 * (pert / pmax)
                    x -= x.mean()
                    m.fill(0.0)
                    v.fill(0.0)
                    t = 0
                stall = 0

    return best, best_f


def _lp_refine(a_in, stop_time, target_score=1.45):
    a = np.asarray(a_in, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    a = _normalize(a)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return a, float("inf")

    best = a.copy()
    best_f = _score(a)

    if n > 1024:
        seq, sc = _adam_optimize(a, stop_time)
        if sc < best_f:
            best, best_f = seq, sc
        return best, best_f

    max_lp_calls = 6000
    if 513 <= n <= 768:
        max_lp_calls = 1800
    elif 769 <= n <= 1024:
        max_lp_calls = 700

    factors = (0.98, 0.96, 0.99, 0.97, 0.95, 0.98, 0.94, 0.97)
    idx = 0
    it = 0
    stall = 0
    lp_calls = 0

    while time.monotonic() < stop_time - 5.0:
        it += 1

        b = _conv(a, a)
        f = _score_from_conv(a, b)
        if f < best_f:
            best_f = f
            best = a.copy()
            stall = 0

        if f > best_f * 1.05:
            a = best.copy()
            b = _conv(a, a)
            f = _score_from_conv(a, b)

        S = float(a.sum())
        M = float(b.max())
        if M <= 0:
            break

        if best_f > 1.45:
            goal_score = best_f * 0.999
        else:
            goal_score = min(1.45, best_f * 0.999)
        goal_M = goal_score * (S * S) / (2.0 * float(n))

        use_lp = (lp_calls < max_lp_calls)
        safe_margin = 20.0 if n <= 512 else 55.0
        if use_lp and time.monotonic() > stop_time - safe_margin:
            use_lp = False

        if not use_lp:
            seq, sc = _adam_optimize(a, stop_time)
            if sc < best_f:
                best, best_f = seq, sc
            break

        factor = factors[idx]
        idx = (idx + 1) % len(factors)

        mt = M * factor
        if goal_M > 0.0:
            mt = min(mt, goal_M)
        floor = 0.92 * M
        if mt < floor:
            mt = floor
        if mt <= 0.0:
            mt = floor

        g = _lp_solve(a, mt)
        lp_calls += 1

        improved = False
        if g is not None:
            Sg = float(g.sum())
            if Sg > 1e-12:
                g = g / Sg
                a_new, f_new = _line_search(a, g, f)
                if f_new < f - 1e-12:
                    a = a_new
                    f = f_new
                    improved = True
                if f_new < best_f - 1e-12:
                    best_f = f_new
                    best = a_new.copy()

        if not improved and (it % 2 == 0) and lp_calls < max_lp_calls:
            mt2 = max(goal_M, 0.995 * M) if goal_M > 0 else 0.995 * M
            if mt2 <= 0:
                mt2 = 0.995 * M
            g2 = _lp_solve(a, mt2)
            lp_calls += 1
            if g2 is not None:
                Sg2 = float(g2.sum())
                if Sg2 > 1e-12:
                    g2 = g2 / Sg2
                    a2, f2 = _line_search(a, g2, f)
                    if f2 < f - 1e-12:
                        a = a2
                        f = f2
                        improved = True
                    if f2 < best_f - 1e-12:
                        best_f = f2
                        best = a2.copy()

        if not improved:
            stall += 1
            if stall == 20 or stall % 50 == 0:
                ag, fg = _gradient_step(a, tau_factor=0.05, lr=0.5)
                if fg < f:
                    a = ag
                    f = fg
                if fg < best_f:
                    best_f = fg
                    best = ag.copy()
                stall = 0

            if stall >= 120:
                r = np.random.default_rng(1234567 + 100000 * n + it)
                v = r.random(n) + 0.05
                v = _normalize(v)
                a = _normalize(0.85 * best + 0.15 * v)
                b = _conv(a, a)
                f = _score_from_conv(a, b)
                stall = 0

    return best, best_f


def _make_central_binomial(n):
    n = int(n)
    c = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 0:
        return c
    c[0] = 1.0
    for k in range(n - 1):
        c[k + 1] = c[k] * (2.0 * k + 1.0) / (2.0 * k + 2.0)
    return _normalize(c)


def _make_power_law(n, alpha=0.48):
    n = int(n)
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
    a = np.power(i, -float(alpha))
    return _normalize(np.maximum(a, 1e-12))


def _make_mixed(n, weight=0.05):
    n = int(n)
    c = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 0:
        return c
    c[0] = 1.0
    for k in range(n - 1):
        c[k + 1] = c[k] * (2.0 * k + 1.0) / (2.0 * k + 2.0)
    a = c + float(weight)
    return _normalize(np.maximum(a, 1e-12))


def _make_resample(seq, n):
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(n)
    m = len(seq)
    if m == 0:
        return _make_central_binomial(n)
    if m == n:
        return _normalize(np.maximum(seq, 0.0))
    xu = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    xn = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    y = np.interp(xn, xu, np.maximum(seq, 0.0))
    return _normalize(y)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    available = max(120.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + available - 60.0

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    # Load the previously known good sequence if available.
    try:
        gs = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if gs.size > 0:
            gs = np.where(np.isfinite(gs), gs, 0.0)
            gs = np.clip(gs, 0.0, 1000.0)
            s = float(gs.sum())
            if s > 1e-12:
                cand = gs / s
                sc = _score(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_seq = cand.copy()
    except Exception:
        pass

    if best_seq is None or len(best_seq) == 0:
        best_seq = _make_central_binomial(512)
        best_score = _score(best_seq)

    # Keep initial length manageable.
    if len(best_seq) > 4096:
        best_seq = _make_resample(best_seq, 2048)
        best_score = _score(best_seq)

    print(f"Initial best: n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.10f}")

    # Phase 1: strengthen the known best / initial best.
    n0 = len(best_seq)
    phase1_budget = 520.0 if n0 <= 1024 else 220.0
    if best_score < 1.50:
        phase1_budget *= 0.65
    stop1 = min(deadline, t0 + phase1_budget)
    if n0 <= 1024:
        seq, sc = _lp_refine(best_seq, stop1, target_score=1.45)
    else:
        seq, sc = _adam_optimize(best_seq, stop1)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
        best_score = sc
        best_seq = seq.copy()
    print(f"After phase1: n={len(best_seq)} score={best_score:.10f}")

    # Build alternative starts.
    starts = []
    base_len = len(best_seq)
    lengths = (512, 768, 1024, 1536, 2048)
    for n in lengths:
        starts.append(_make_central_binomial(n))
        if n != base_len:
            starts.append(_make_resample(best_seq, n))
        else:
            starts.append(_make_mixed(n, weight=0.05))

    starts.append(_make_power_law(512, alpha=0.48))
    starts.append(_make_power_law(1024, alpha=0.48))

    # Sort by length to spend cheap searches first.
    starts = sorted(starts, key=lambda x: len(x))

    for start in starts:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 80.0:
            break

        n = len(start)
        if n == len(best_seq):
            diff = start - best_seq
            if float(np.linalg.norm(diff)) / max(1, n) < 1e-6:
                continue

        if n <= 512:
            alloc = 70.0
        elif n <= 1024:
            alloc = 110.0
        elif n <= 2048:
            alloc = 140.0
        else:
            alloc = 120.0

        if best_score < 1.48:
            alloc *= 0.5
        elif best_score > 1.51:
            alloc *= 1.2

        remaining = deadline - 60.0 - time.monotonic()
        alloc = min(alloc, remaining)
        if alloc < 25.0:
            continue

        stop = time.monotonic() + alloc
        if n <= 1024:
            seq, sc = _lp_refine(start, stop, target_score=1.45)
        else:
            seq, sc = _adam_optimize(start, stop)

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = seq.copy()
            print(f"New best from start n={n}: score={best_score:.10f}")

    # Final focused refinement.
    if best_seq is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 10.0:
        # If a longer sequence is best, try a lower-dimensional projection that can use LP.
        if len(best_seq) > 1024 and deadline - time.monotonic() > 140.0:
            cand = _make_resample(best_seq, 1024)
            stop = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 180.0)
            seq, sc = _lp_refine(cand, stop, target_score=1.45)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_seq = seq.copy()
                print(f"Resample 1024 improved: score={best_score:.10f}")

        # Refine the current global best with remaining time.
        if len(best_seq) <= 1024:
            seq, sc = _lp_refine(best_seq, deadline, target_score=1.45)
        else:
            seq, sc = _adam_optimize(best_seq, deadline)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = seq.copy()

        # Short final cross-check at length 512, often LP-friendly.
        if len(best_seq) != 512 and deadline - time.monotonic() > 70.0:
            cand = _make_resample(best_seq, 512)
            stop = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 80.0)
            seq, sc = _lp_refine(cand, stop, target_score=1.45)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_seq = seq.copy()
                print(f"Final 512 resample improved: score={best_score:.10f}")

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = _make_central_binomial(512)

    out = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        out = _make_central_binomial(512)
        s = float(out.sum())
    out = out / s

    final_score = _score(out)
    print(f"Final internal score: n={len(out)} score={final_score:.10f}")
    try:
        ev = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in out]))
        print(f"Final evaluate_sequence score: {ev:.10f}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"evaluate_sequence check failed: {e}")

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
